### assistant 2026-05-06T23:04:28.204Z

Yeah, I get exactly what you mean.

Youâ€™re asking for **note/file archetypes**: recurring documentation objects like PRDs, contracts, prompts, specs, ADRs, handoff docs, etc. For your work, Obsidian should become less â€œnotes appâ€ and more **personal product/engineering knowledge base**.

A good organizing principle:

> **Every file should either capture intent, define a contract, explain a decision, guide execution, record state, or preserve reusable knowledge.**

Modern documentation practice also supports this split: DiÃ¡taxis separates docs into tutorials, how-to guides, reference, and explanation; ADR practice separates durable decisions from general docs; OpenAPI formalizes machine-readable API contracts; GitHub community files establish common repo-level documentation conventions. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn449459search1îˆ‚turn449459search2îˆ‚turn449459search3îˆ‚turn449459search0îˆ

## The main file types you commonly generate

### 1. Product / business intent files

These explain **what the product is and why it exists**.

Common types:

- **Product Brief**
- **PRD**
- **MVP Scope**
- **Feature Brief**
- **User Stories**
- **User Flows**
- **Launch Copy**
- **Positioning Memo**
- **ICP / Persona Notes**
- **Market Thesis**
- **Competitor Analysis**
- **Pricing Model**
- **Monetization Plan**
- **Risk Register**
- **Roadmap**
- **Release Criteria**
- **Go-to-Market Plan**
- **Demo Script**
- **Pitch Notes**
- **Naming / Branding Notes**
- **Design Principles**

For your projects, these are the human-facing â€œwhy/whatâ€ layer.

Example names:

```txt
prd.md
mvp-scope.md
user-flows.md
launch-copy.md
pricing-model.md
positioning.md
risk-register.md
roadmap.md
```

---

### 2. Domain model / contract files

These are some of your most important files. They define the **truth of the system**.

Common types:

- **Domain Model**
- **Domain Contract**
- **Entity Catalog**
- **Relationship Map**
- **State Machine**
- **Lifecycle Contract**
- **Role Contract**
- **Permission Contract**
- **Authorization Matrix**
- **Readiness Gates**
- **Acceptance Gates**
- **Business Rules**
- **Invariant List**
- **Validation Rules**
- **Error Taxonomy**
- **Workflow Contract**
- **Event Contract**
- **Audit Contract**
- **Analytics Contract**
- **Notification Contract**
- **Billing Contract**
- **Integration Contract**

Example names:

```txt
domain-model.md
domain-contract.md
roles.md
authz-matrix.md
state-machine.md
readiness-gates.md
acceptance-gates.md
business-rules.md
analytics-contract.md
billing-contract.md
```

For your `.agents/contracts` style, many of these can also exist as machine-readable YAML:

```txt
domain-model.yaml
roles.yaml
authz.yaml
routes.yaml
features.yaml
analytics.yaml
acceptance-gates.yaml
```

---

### 3. Architecture / engineering design files

These explain **how the system is built**.

Common types:

- **System Architecture Overview**
- **Technical Requirements Document**
- **Architecture Decision Record**
- **Architecture Decision Log**
- **Data Flow Diagram Note**
- **Service Boundary Spec**
- **Module Boundary Spec**
- **Layering Rules**
- **Server/Client Boundary Rules**
- **Database Architecture**
- **Auth Architecture**
- **Payment Architecture**
- **Cache Strategy**
- **Queue / Job Strategy**
- **Webhook Strategy**
- **Error Handling Strategy**
- **Observability Strategy**
- **Security Model**
- **Deployment Architecture**
- **Environment Strategy**
- **Scalability Notes**
- **Migration Plan**
- **Refactor Plan**
- **Dependency Analysis**

ADRs are especially useful because they capture one significant decision, the context, the alternatives rejected, and the consequences. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn449459search6îˆ‚turn449459search14îˆ

Example names:

```txt
architecture-overview.md
technical-requirements.md
adr-0001-use-neon-postgres.md
adr-0002-use-clerk-auth.md
server-client-boundaries.md
cache-strategy.md
webhook-strategy.md
deployment-architecture.md
```

---

These bridge product intent and actual code.

- **Implementation Plan**
- **Route Spec**
- **Page Spec**
- **Component Spec**
- **Form Spec**

- **Empty State Spec**

- **Error State Spec**
- **Permission Behavior Spec**

Example names:

route-spec.md
component-spec.md
form-spec.md
action-spec.md

```

```txt
app route â†’ feature shell â†’ fetchers/actions â†’ schemas â†’ types â†’ db/selects â†’ db/transactions
```

### 5. Agent / Codex / automation files

This is a category you use more than most people, and it deserves first-class treatment.

Common types:

- **Agent Instructions**
- **Codex Mega Prompt**
- **Codex Work Package**
- **Patch Prompt**
- **Audit Prompt**
- **Refactor Prompt**
- **Enforcement Prompt**
- **Bugfix Prompt**
- **Handoff Prompt**
- **Repo Orientation Prompt**
- **Scaffold Prompt**
- **Implementation Contract**
- **Stop Conditions**
- **Verification Checklist**
- **Post-Run Report Template**
- **Regression Sweep Prompt**
- **Prompt Changelog**
- **Known Agent Failure Modes**
- **Tooling Instructions**
- **MCP Setup Notes**
- **Model Usage Strategy**
- **Rate Limit Strategy**

Example names:

```txt
codex-work-package.md
codex-mega-prompt.md
post-refactor-audit-prompt.md
boundary-enforcement-prompt.md
handoff-prompt.md
agent-instructions.md
stop-conditions.md
verification-checklist.md
known-agent-failures.md
```

Your `.agents` structure already points toward this split:

```txt
.agents/docs/          human intent
.agents/contracts/     machine-readable truth
.agents/instructions/  agent interpretation rules
.agents/execution/     state, reports, backlog, handoff
.agents/prompts/       reusable agent commands
```

That is worth preserving.

### 6. Repo / project governance files

These define how the project is maintained.

Common types:

- **README**
- **Contributing Guide**
- **Security Policy**
- **Support Policy**
- **License Notes**
- **Code of Conduct**
- **Changelog**
- **Release Notes**

- **Branching Strategy**
- **Commit Convention**
- **Definition of Done**
- **Definition of Ready**
- **Engineering Standards**
- **Review Checklist**
- **Testing Policy**
- **Deprecation Policy**

GitHub treats files like README, LICENSE, CONTRIBUTING, CODE_OF_CONDUCT, SECURITY, and SUPPORT as common â€œcommunity healthâ€ files for repositories. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn449459search0îˆ‚turn449459search4îˆ

Example names:

This is probably the biggest unlock for you in Obsidian. You do not need to backlink everything. Just backlink the durable nouns:

```txt

[[RateLtd]]
[[Stripe Connect]]
[[Clerk]]
[[Domain Model]]
[[AuthZ]]
[[ADR]]
[[Codex Work Package]]

### assistant 2026-05-07T00:11:12.109Z

Yeah â€” that confusion is on the wording.

When I said **contracts**, I did **not** mean OpenAI, OpenAPI, legal contracts, or API contracts specifically.

I meant:

> **Project contract files:** plain Markdown/YAML documents that define the rules Codex, ChatGPT, and future-you must obey when building a project.

They are like **source-of-truth specs** for a repo.

## Example: `authz.yaml`

For Vouch, a contract might look like this:

```yaml
contract: authz
project: Vouch
version: 0.1.0
status: active

principles:
  - Users may only access vouches where they are an active participant.
  - Payers and payees have different setup requirements.
  - Admin/system actions must never be reachable from public UI routes.

roles:
  payer:
    description: User funding the vouch.
    can:
      - create_vouch
      - invite_payee
      - confirm_presence
      - view_own_vouches
    cannot:
      - approve_own_payout
      - access_other_user_vouches

  payee:
    description: User accepting the commitment-backed payment.
    can:
      - accept_vouch
      - complete_payout_setup
      - confirm_presence
      - view_own_vouches
    cannot:
      - create_payment_authorization
      - access_other_user_vouches

guards:
  create_vouch:
    requires:
      - authenticated_user
      - adult_verified
      - identity_verified
      - payment_method_ready
      - terms_accepted

  accept_vouch:
    requires:
      - authenticated_user
      - adult_verified
      - identity_verified
      - payout_account_ready
      - terms_accepted

  confirm_presence:
    requires:
      - authenticated_user
      - active_participant
      - vouch_active
      - confirmation_window_open
      - no_prior_confirmation_for_role
```

That file becomes a **contract** because Codex is not allowed to freestyle around it.

It tells the agent:

```txt
Before writing actions, routes, middleware, UI states, or tests, obey this authority model.
```

## Example: `features.yaml`

```yaml
contract: features
project: RateLtd
version: 0.1.0
status: active

features:
  command_palette:
    description: Global searchable launcher for all TUI actions.
    required: true
    routes:
      - /commands
    modules:
      - command-index
      - command-runner
      - command-history
    acceptance:
      - User can search commands by name.
      - User can execute a command.
      - User can view recent commands.
      - Failed commands surface stderr and exit code.

  git_diff_viewer:
    description: Terminal-native diff explorer.
    required: true
    modules:
      - repo-scanner
      - diff-parser
      - diff-renderer
    acceptance:
      - User can select a repository.
      - User can view changed files.
      - User can inspect line-level diffs.
      - User can stage or discard changes only after confirmation.
```

This is not OpenAPI. It is closer to a **repo constitution**.

## Example: `domain-contract.md`

```md
# Vouch Domain Contract

## Product Definition

Vouch is a commitment-backed payment coordination tool for users who already have a pre-arranged agreement.

Vouch is not:
- an escrow company
- a marketplace
- a broker
- a dispute resolution service
- a lending product

## Core Domain Objects

### User

A registered account holder authenticated through Clerk.

### Vouch

A commitment-backed payment agreement between two verified users.

### Confirmation

A time-windowed presence signal submitted by one participant.

### Payment Authorization

A provider-backed manual-capture authorization created before final release.

## Core Rule

Funds may only release when both participants confirm presence during the valid confirmation window.

If confirmation fails, expires, or remains incomplete, the default outcome is non-release.
```

That kind of file is extremely useful because it prevents agent drift.

## The better term

Instead of â€œOpenAI contracts,â€ use one of these:

```txt
project contracts
agent contracts
execution contracts
domain contracts
implementation contracts
governance specs
source-of-truth specs
```

## The Obsidian version

For your vault, I would organize around this:

```txt
PROJECTS/
  RateLtd/
    contracts/
      product.yaml
      features.yaml
      architecture.yaml
      modules.yaml
      commands.yaml
      acceptance-gates.yaml
      codex-rules.yaml

  Vouch/
    contracts/
      product.yaml
      domain.yaml
      authz.yaml
      readiness-gates.yaml
      payment-lifecycle.yaml
      settlement-state-machine.yaml
      acceptance-gates.yaml
      codex-rules.yaml
```

## Clean definition

A **contract file** is:

> A durable spec that defines what must be true, what must not happen, and how implementation should be judged.

It is not a note.  
It is not a brainstorm.  
It is not documentation after the fact.

It is a **control surface** for building software with agents.

### assistant 2026-05-07T00:14:14.984Z

Exactly. Youâ€™re asking:

> **How do we decide what bucket something belongs in, what itâ€™s called, and where it lives?**

Hereâ€™s the clean model.

## Core idea

A contract gets its identity from four things:

```txt
scope + project + domain + contract-kind
```

Example:

```txt
project.vouch.payments.settlement-state-machine
project.rateltd.commands.command-registry
project.ctrlplus.visualizer.catalog-pipeline
vault.obsidian.notes.note-taxonomy
```

That becomes the **namespace**.

The file path is just the filesystem version of that namespace.

```txt
PROJECTS/Vouch/contracts/payments/settlement-state-machine.yaml
PROJECTS/RateLtd/contracts/commands/command-registry.yaml
OBSIDIAN/contracts/notes/note-taxonomy.yaml
```

## The terms

### 1. Scope

The level of authority.

```txt
vault       applies to your entire Obsidian system
project     applies to one project
domain      applies to one bounded business/technical area
feature     applies to one feature
module      applies to one implementation module
operation   applies to a workflow, runbook, or Codex execution
reference   reusable knowledge, not project authority
archive     deprecated / historical
```

Examples:

```txt
vault.obsidian.notes.taxonomy
project.vouch.product.positioning
domain.vouch.payments.lifecycle
feature.rateltd.git-diff-viewer.behavior
operation.codex.rateltd.implementation-pass
```

---

### 2. Project

The product or repo boundary.

For you:

```txt
vouch
rateltd
ctrlplus
meatharness
borderpulse
codependentcoding
devnotes
```

Project answers:

> â€œWhat product/system owns this truth?â€

Example:

```yaml
project: vouch
```

---

### 3. Domain

The area where rules belong together.

A domain is **not just a folder name**. It is a zone of meaning where the same nouns, rules, states, and permissions keep showing up together.

For **Vouch**, domains might be:

```txt
product
identity
users
vouches
payments
payouts
confirmations
settlement
readiness
authz
legal
messaging
dashboard
settings
agent-ops
```

For **RateLtd**, domains might be:

```txt
product
tui-shell
commands
filesystem
repositories
git-diffs
powershell
termcn-ui
monaco
settings
sessions
task-runner
agent-ops
packaging
```

For **CtrlPlus**, domains might be:

```txt
product
tenancy
authz
customers
vehicles
wrap-catalog
visualizer
media
cloudinary
huggingface
billing
dashboard
agent-ops
```

Domain answers:

> â€œWhat part of the system does this contract govern?â€

---

### 4. Contract kind

The type of truth being defined.

Common contract kinds:

```txt
product-definition
domain-model
state-machine
lifecycle
roles
authz
readiness-gates
acceptance-gates
feature-inventory
route-map
module-spec
data-model
api-contract
event-contract
webhook-contract
integration-contract
ui-contract
copy-contract
test-contract
codex-rules
work-package
verification-checklist
handoff
```

Contract kind answers:

> â€œWhat shape of rule is this?â€

---

## The naming formula

Use this:

```txt
{scope}.{project}.{domain}.{contract-kind}
```

Examples:

```txt
project.vouch.payments.lifecycle
project.vouch.authz.roles
project.vouch.confirmations.state-machine
project.vouch.readiness.acceptance-gates

project.rateltd.commands.command-registry
project.rateltd.tui-shell.layout-contract
project.rateltd.git-diffs.module-spec
project.rateltd.agent-ops.codex-work-package

project.ctrlplus.visualizer.golden-prototype-contract
project.ctrlplus.wrap-catalog.data-model
project.ctrlplus.media.integration-contract
```

## Matching file paths

Mirror the namespace, but keep folders readable:

```txt
PROJECTS/
  Vouch/
    contracts/
      payments/
        lifecycle.yaml
        settlement-state-machine.yaml
      confirmations/
        confirmation-window.yaml
        pin-fallback.yaml
      authz/
        roles.yaml
        permissions.yaml
      readiness/
        gates.yaml

  RateLtd/
    contracts/
      commands/
        command-registry.yaml
        command-execution.yaml
      tui-shell/
        layout-contract.yaml
        navigation-contract.yaml
      git-diffs/
        module-spec.yaml
      agent-ops/
        codex-work-package.yaml
        verification-checklist.yaml
```

## Example contract header

Every contract should start with the same metadata block:

```yaml
id: project.vouch.payments.settlement-state-machine
kind: state-machine
scope: project
project: vouch
domain: payments
namespace: project.vouch.payments
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
version: 0.1.0

appliesTo:
  routes:
    - app/(tenant)/vouches/[vouchId]/page.tsx
  actions:
    - lib/actions/paymentActions.ts
    - lib/actions/vouchActions.ts
  fetchers:
    - lib/fetchers/paymentFetchers.ts
  schemas:
    - schemas/payment.ts
  types:
    - types/payment.ts

dependsOn:
  - project.vouch.authz.roles
  - project.vouch.readiness.gates
  - project.vouch.confirmations.state-machine

supersedes: []
```

Then the body defines the actual rules:

```yaml
states:
  - pending_authorization
  - authorized
  - confirmation_open
  - dual_confirmed
  - captured
  - voided
  - refunded
  - expired

invariants:
  - Funds must not capture unless both participants confirmed presence.
  - Failed or incomplete confirmation defaults to non-release.
  - Provider state must be reconciled before capture, void, or refund.

transitions:
  authorize:
    from: pending_authorization
    to: authorized
    requires:
      - payer_payment_ready
      - active_vouch
      - terms_accepted

  capture:
    from: dual_confirmed
    to: captured
    requires:
      - payer_confirmed
      - payee_confirmed
      - provider_authorization_valid
      - idempotency_key
```

## The decision rule

When deciding the domain, ask:

```txt
1. What noun is this mostly about?
2. What rules would break if this changed?
3. Which code modules must obey it?
4. Does it define business truth, implementation structure, UI behavior, or agent behavior?
5. Would another project reuse it, or is it project-specific?
```

Examples:

```txt
â€œFunds release only after both people confirm.â€
â†’ project.vouch.settlement.state-machine

â€œPayee needs payout readiness before accepting.â€
â†’ project.vouch.readiness.gates

â€œCodex must not put Prisma in UI components.â€
â†’ project.global.architecture.boundary-rules
or project.vouch.agent-ops.codex-rules

â€œRateLtd command palette must run real PowerShell commands.â€
â†’ project.rateltd.commands.execution-contract

â€œTermCN layout regions and navigation behavior.â€
â†’ project.rateltd.tui-shell.layout-contract
```

## Split vs merge rule

Split contracts when:

```txt
- different actors are involved
- different state machines are involved
- different code owners/modules obey them
- one file would become too broad
- rules change at different speeds
```

Merge contracts when:

```txt
- rules always change together
- they describe one lifecycle
- separating them would create duplicate references
- the distinction is only aesthetic
```

Example for Vouch:

```txt
Do not merge:
payments + confirmations + authz

Because:
payments = provider money movement
confirmations = presence signals
authz = who may act

But connect them through dependsOn.
```

## The clean mental model

Think of every contract as having an address:

```txt
project.vouch.payments.lifecycle
```

That address means:

```txt
scope: project
project: vouch
domain: payments
kind: lifecycle
```

Then the path becomes obvious:

```txt
PROJECTS/Vouch/contracts/payments/lifecycle.yaml
```

And the Obsidian title becomes obvious:

```txt
Vouch Payments Lifecycle Contract
```

## The practical standard Iâ€™d use for you

For every serious project:

```txt
contracts/
  product/
    definition.yaml
    positioning.yaml
    scope.yaml

  domain/
    model.yaml
    glossary.yaml
    invariants.yaml

  authz/
    roles.yaml
    permissions.yaml

  architecture/
    boundaries.yaml
    route-map.yaml
    data-flow.yaml

  features/
    inventory.yaml
    acceptance-gates.yaml

  agent-ops/
    codex-rules.yaml
    work-packages.yaml
    verification-checklist.yaml
```

Then add domain-specific folders only when the project needs them:

```txt
Vouch:
  payments/
  payouts/
  confirmations/
  readiness/
  settlement/
  legal/

RateLtd:
  tui-shell/
  commands/
  powershell/
  git-diffs/
  filesystem/
  termcn-ui/
  monaco/
```

That is the dot-connecting piece:

> **Domain is the rule boundary. Namespace is the contractâ€™s stable address. File path is the namespace rendered onto disk.**

Yes. Iâ€™d do this, but Iâ€™d rename the organizing principle to avoid the OpenAPI/OpenAI confusion:

> **Contract Object Model**  
> A vault-wide schema for deciding what a note is, where it lives, what authority it has, and what other notes/contracts it depends on.

You already have the right plugins for this: Templater, QuickAdd, Dataview, Tasks, Linter, Note Refactor, Text Transporter, Advanced Canvas, Calendar, and Table Editor are visible in the vault tree. îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn2file7îˆ Your current project structure also gives us real targets: CtrlPlus, MeatHarness, RateLtd, Vouch, BorderPulse, and CodependentCoding. îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn2file8îˆ

Also: yes, Obsidian now has an official CLI. It can be enabled from **Settings â†’ General â†’ Command line interface**, and Obsidian says the app must be running for CLI commands to work. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn299424search0îˆ‚turn299424search3îˆ

## My proposed approach

### Phase 0 â€” Audit, no moves yet

First we inventory the vault:

```txt
notes
folders
frontmatter
tags
existing templates
plugins
duplicate-ish files
project folders
empty/untitled/orphan notes
contract-like notes
research notes
reference docs
creative/writing notes
```

No migration. No deletion. No destructive automation.

---

### Phase 1 â€” Define the vault schema

Create one canonical schema for every important note type:

```txt
product-definition
domain-model
state-machine
lifecycle
roles
authz
readiness-gates
acceptance-gates
feature-inventory
route-map
module-spec
data-model
api-contract
event-contract
webhook-contract
integration-contract
ui-contract
copy-contract
test-contract
codex-rules
work-package
verification-checklist
handoff
research-report
reference-doc
execution-log
decision-record
```

Every note gets a consistent property envelope:

```yaml
---
id:
title:
type:
contract_kind:
scope:
project:
domain:
namespace:
status:
authority:
parents:
depends_on:
supersedes:
tags:
created:
updated:
---
```

Not every note becomes a contract. Some are research, reference, creative writing, execution logs, or archives.

---

### Phase 2 â€” Build templates

We create professional-quality templates in:

## One correction to your proposal

This part needs adjustment:

> â€œapply templates to all filesâ€

I would not apply full templates to every existing note. That will wreck notes that are already useful.

Better:

## First command: gather context

Run this from the root of your vault, likely `DevNotes/`.

```powershell
$VaultRoot = (Get-Location).Path
$Stamp = Get-Date -Format "yyyyMMdd-HHmmss"
$OutDir = Join-Path $VaultRoot "_OPS\vault-audit\$Stamp"
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $OutDir | Out-Null

$IgnoreDirs = @(
  ".obsidian\plugins",
  ".trash",
  "_OPS"
)

function Get-RelativePath {
  param([string]$FullPath)
  return $FullPath.Substring($VaultRoot.Length).TrimStart('\','/')
}

function Test-Ignored {
  param([string]$RelativePath)
  foreach ($dir in $IgnoreDirs) {
    if ($RelativePath -like "$dir*") { return $true }
  }
  return $false
}

function Get-FrontmatterValue {
  param(
    [string]$Yaml,
    [string]$Key
  )
  $match = [regex]::Match($Yaml, "(?m)^$([regex]::Escape($Key)):\s*(?<v>.*)$")
  if ($match.Success) { return $match.Groups["v"].Value.Trim().Trim('"') }
  return $null
}

$MarkdownFiles = Get-ChildItem -Path $VaultRoot -Recurse -File -Filter "*.md" |
  Where-Object {
    $rel = Get-RelativePath $_.FullName
    -not (Test-Ignored $rel)
  }

$Notes = foreach ($file in $MarkdownFiles) {
  $rel = Get-RelativePath $file.FullName
  $text = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $file.FullName
  $hash = (Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 -LiteralPath $file.FullName).Hash

  $fm = [regex]::Match($text, "^---\s*\r?\n(?<yaml>[\s\S]*?)\r?\n---", "Multiline")
  $yaml = if ($fm.Success) { $fm.Groups["yaml"].Value } else { "" }

  $h1 = [regex]::Match($text, "(?m)^#\s+(?<h>.+)$")
  $title = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "title"
  if (-not $title -and $h1.Success) { $title = $h1.Groups["h"].Value.Trim() }
  if (-not $title) { $title = [IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($file.Name) }

  [pscustomobject]@{
    relativePath  = $rel
    fileName      = $file.Name
    folder        = Split-Path $rel -Parent
    title         = $title
    sizeBytes     = $file.Length
    lineCount     = (($text -split "`r?`n").Count)
    wordCount     = (($text -split "\s+" | Where-Object { $_ }).Count)
    hasFrontmatter = $fm.Success
    type          = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "type"
    project       = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "project"
    domain        = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "domain"
    contract_kind = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "contract_kind"
    status        = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "status"
    tagsRaw       = Get-FrontmatterValue $yaml "tags"
    sha256        = $hash
    lastWriteTime = $file.LastWriteTime.ToString("o")
  }
}

$Plugins = Get-ChildItem -Path (Join-Path $VaultRoot ".obsidian\plugins") -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
  ForEach-Object {
    $manifest = Join-Path $_.FullName "manifest.json"
    $data = if (Test-Path $manifest) { Get-Content -Raw $manifest | ConvertFrom-Json } else { $null }

    [pscustomobject]@{
      folder = $_.Name
      name = if ($data) { $data.name } else { $null }
      id = if ($data) { $data.id } else { $_.Name }
      version = if ($data) { $data.version } else { $null }
    }
  }

$Duplicates = $Notes |
  Group-Object sha256 |
  Where-Object { $_.Count -gt 1 } |
  ForEach-Object {
    [pscustomobject]@{
      sha256 = $_.Name
      count = $_.Count
      files = ($_.Group.relativePath -join " | ")
    }
  }

$Notes | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6 | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "notes.json")
$Notes | Export-Csv -NoTypeInformation -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "notes.csv")
$Plugins | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6 | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "plugins.json")
$Duplicates | Export-Csv -NoTypeInformation -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "duplicates.csv")

tree $VaultRoot /F /A | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "tree.txt")

$Summary = @"
# Vault Audit Summary

Vault: $VaultRoot
Created: $(Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss")

## Counts

- Markdown notes: $($Notes.Count)
- Plugins: $($Plugins.Count)
- Exact duplicate groups: $($Duplicates.Count)

## Output Files

- notes.json
- notes.csv
- plugins.json
- duplicates.csv
- tree.txt
"@

$Summary | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 (Join-Path $OutDir "audit-summary.md")

Write-Host ""
Write-Host "Vault audit complete:" -ForegroundColor Green
Write-Host $OutDir
Write-Host ""
Get-Content (Join-Path $OutDir "audit-summary.md")
```

Then send me the generated:

```txt
_OPS/vault-audit/<timestamp>/audit-summary.md
_OPS/vault-audit/<timestamp>/notes.csv
_OPS/vault-audit/<timestamp>/duplicates.csv
```

After that, Iâ€™ll give you the first JSON write payload for the schema, templates, and dashboard notes.

## Proposed top-level tree

## Project namespace rule

Not every project needs every folder. But the shape is predictable.

## Breadcrumb model

Every serious note gets properties like this:

```yaml
---
title: RateLtd Command Registry Contract
type: contract
scope: project
project: RateLtd
domain: commands
contract_kind: command-registry
namespace: PROJECTS.PRO-RateLtd.CONTRACTS.commands.command-registry
parent: "[[PRO-RateLtd]]"
breadcrumb:
  - "10 PROJECTS"
  - "PRO-RateLtd"
  - "20 CONTRACTS"
  - "commands"
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
tags:
  - projects/rateltd
  - contracts/commands
  - status/active
---
```

That gives you the breadcrumb behavior without needing weak folder names.

## Naming rule

Folders can be structured. Files stay human.

Good:

```txt

Bad:

### assistant 2026-05-07T01:02:18.945Z

Yes â€” **kind of**.

Obsidian lets you use dot notation in filenames just fine:

```txt
rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
ctrlplus.visualizer.pipeline.contract.md
```

But Obsidian does **not** natively treat dot notation as a formal hierarchy the way Dendron does. Dendron explicitly organizes notes into dot-delimited hierarchies like `project1.tasks.task1.md`; that hierarchy is a first-class Dendron concept. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn246505search0îˆ

In Obsidian, dot notation is just a filename convention â€” but it still works extremely well because Obsidian search, links, tags, properties, Dataview, and CLI commands can all operate around that convention.

## So the answer is:

```txt
Dendron:
dot notation = native hierarchy system

Obsidian:
dot notation = powerful naming convention
```

That means you can absolutely run a Dendron-style workflow in Obsidian, but **you have to define the rules yourself**.

## The amoeba pattern works here

The workflow you described is real:

```txt
scratch note
â†’ highlight useful section
â†’ extract into new atomic note
â†’ replace original section with link
â†’ new note joins the namespace
```

Obsidian has a core plugin called **Note Composer** that can extract selected text into another note or into a new note. The command is **â€œNote composer: Extract current selectionâ€¦â€**. îˆ€citeîˆ‚turn606434search1îˆ

You also have CLI support for commands, creating files, moving files, reading files, searching, setting properties, templates, QuickAdd, backlinks, links, tags, orphans, and unresolved links. îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn4file0îˆ

So your Obsidian version of Dendron-style note growth would be:

```txt
00 ZETTELKASTEN/scratch.md

highlight section:
"RateLtd needs a command registry contract..."

extract to:
rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md

original note becomes:
See [[rateltd.commands.registry.contract]]
```

## Recommended dot notation for your vault

Use:

```txt
<project>.<domain>.<artifact>.<kind>.md
```

Examples:

```txt
rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
rateltd.commands.execution.contract.md
rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract.md
rateltd.termcn.component-map.reference.md
rateltd.codex.work-package.md

vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
vouch.payments.settlement.state-machine.md
vouch.authz.roles.contract.md
vouch.readiness.gates.contract.md
vouch.legal.terms.reference.md

ctrlplus.visualizer.pipeline.contract.md
ctrlplus.wrap-catalog.data-model.contract.md
ctrlplus.architecture.boundaries.contract.md
```

## How folders fit in

Use folders for **big shelves**, dot notation for **actual identity**.

```txt
10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/
  rateltd.product.vision.md
  rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
  rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract.md

10 PROJECTS/Vouch/
  vouch.product.definition.md
  vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
  vouch.authz.roles.contract.md
```

That gives you the best of both worlds:

```txt
folders = physical neighborhood
dot notation = semantic address
properties = machine-readable metadata
links = knowledge graph
```

## CLI examples

Create a Dendron-style contract note:

```powershell
obsidian create path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" content="# RateLtd Commands Registry Contract`n`n## Purpose`n`nDefine the canonical command registry for RateLtd.`n"
```

Set properties:

```powershell
obsidian property:set path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" name="type" value="contract" type=text
obsidian property:set path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" name="project" value="RateLtd" type=text
obsidian property:set path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" name="domain" value="commands" type=text
obsidian property:set path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" name="contract_kind" value="registry" type=text
obsidian property:set path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md" name="namespace" value="rateltd.commands.registry.contract" type=text
```

Search the namespace:

```powershell
obsidian search query="rateltd.commands"
```

List links/backlinks:

```powershell
obsidian links path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md"
obsidian backlinks path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md"
```

## Bottom line

Yes, you can use the Dendron dot-notation mindset in Obsidian.

The key difference:

> Obsidian will not enforce the hierarchy for you.  
> But with dot-named files, templates, properties, QuickAdd, Note Composer, Dataview, and CLI commands, you can build a system that feels close to Dendron while staying fully Obsidian-native.

The system:

Johnny.Decimal-style numbering  â†’ top-level folder order
Dendron-style dot notation      â†’ semantic note namespaces
Zettelkasten                    â†’ atomic note growth / extraction
ADR/RFC-style docs              â†’ decision + contract structure
DiÃ¡taxis                        â†’ documentation type discipline

```

## Final naming principle

Folders are shelves.  
Dot notation is identity.  
Properties are metadata.  
Links are graph edges.

Example:

```txt
10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract.md

## Contract naming standard

Use:

```txt
<scope-or-project>.<domain>.<artifact>.<kind>.md
```

Examples:

```txt
rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
rateltd.commands.execution.contract.md
rateltd.termcn.component-map.reference.md
rateltd.codex.work-package.md

vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md

vouch.settlement.state-machine.md

obsidian.contracts.naming-standard.md
obsidian.templates.contract-schema.md
devnotes.zettelkasten.capture-workflow.md
```

## Folder naming standard

Top-level:

```txt
00 ZETTELKASTEN
10 PROJECTS
20 DOCUMENTATION
30 DEEP RESEARCH
40 TECH STACK
50 REGRETS, CIGARETTES, & NEURAL NETS
90 OBSIDIAN
```

Projects:

```txt
10 PROJECTS/
  RateLtd/
  Vouch/
  CtrlPlus/
  MeatHarness/
  BorderPulse/
  CodependentCoding/
```

No `PRO-`. Project name is enough.

## Contract note frontmatter standard

Every serious note gets this shape:

```yaml
---
title:
type:
scope:
project:
domain:
artifact:
kind:
namespace:
status:
authority:
parent:
depends_on:
supersedes:
tags:
created:
updated:
---
```

Example:

```yaml
---
title: RateLtd Commands Registry Contract
type: contract
scope: project
project: RateLtd
domain: commands
artifact: registry
kind: contract
namespace: rateltd.commands.registry.contract
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
parent: "[[rateltd.project.map]]"
depends_on:
  - "[[rateltd.product.vision]]"
  - "[[rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract]]"
supersedes: []
tags:
  - projects/rateltd
  - contracts/commands
  - status/active
created: 2026-05-06
updated: 2026-05-06
---
```

### assistant 2026-05-07T01:08:42.253Z

Copy this whole block into PowerShell **from the root of your vault**. It uses your Obsidian CLI move/create workflow and your existing file inventory as the target map. îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn3file0îˆ îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn3file1îˆ

```powershell
# DevNotes Vault Contract-System Bootstrap
# Run from the root of your Obsidian vault.
# This creates structure, moves obvious files, creates templates, and writes operating docs.
# No deletes. No permanent operations.

Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$VaultRoot = (Get-Location).Path

if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
  throw "Run this from the root of your Obsidian vault. I don't see a .obsidian folder here: $VaultRoot"
}

$ObsCmd = Get-Command obsidian -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
if (-not $ObsCmd) {
  throw "Obsidian CLI not found. Install/enable it first, then rerun this."
}

function Ensure-Dir {
  param([Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path)
  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
    Write-Host "DIR  + $Path" -ForegroundColor DarkGreen
  }
}

function Ensure-Parent {
  param([Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path)
  $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent
  if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent }
}

function Move-FolderSafe {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To
  )

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP folder missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    return
  }

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To)) {
    Ensure-Parent $To
    Move-Item -LiteralPath $From -Destination $To
    Write-Host "MOVE folder: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    return
  }

  Write-Host "MERGE folder contents: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow
  Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $From -Force | ForEach-Object {
    $dest = Join-Path $To $_.Name
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dest)) {
      Move-Item -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Destination $dest
      Write-Host "  MOVE child: $($_.Name)" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    } else {
      Write-Host "  SKIP conflict: $dest" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    }
  }
}

function Move-NoteSafe {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To
  )

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP note missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    return
  }

  if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) {
    Write-Host "SKIP note exists: $To" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    return
  }

  Ensure-Parent $To
  & obsidian move "path=$From" "to=$To" | Out-Host
  Write-Host "MOVE note: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
}

function Write-TextFile {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Content,
    [switch]$Overwrite
  )

  Ensure-Parent $Path

  if ((Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path) -and (-not $Overwrite)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP file exists: $Path" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    return
  }

  $Content | Set-Content -LiteralPath $Path -Encoding UTF8
  Write-Host "WRITE file: $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
}

$namingStandard = @'
---
title: Obsidian Contract Naming Standard
type: standard
scope: vault
domain: obsidian
artifact: naming
kind: standard
namespace: obsidian.contracts.naming-standard
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
tags:
  - obsidian/contracts
  - obsidian/naming
  - status/active
---

# Obsidian Contract Naming Standard

## Core Rule

Use folders for coarse neighborhoods and dot notation for semantic identity.

```txt
<project-or-scope>.<domain>.<artifact>.<kind>.md
```

## Examples

```txt
rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract.md
vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
vouch.authz.roles.contract.md
ctrlplus.visualizer.pipeline.contract.md
obsidian.zettelkasten.capture-workflow.md
```

## Meaning

```txt
project-or-scope = RateLtd, Vouch, CtrlPlus, Obsidian, DevNotes
domain           = payments, authz, commands, tui-shell, visualizer
artifact         = lifecycle, registry, layout, roles, pipeline
kind             = contract, reference, workflow, map, spec, checklist
```

## Folder Rule

Top-level folders use numbered all-caps names.

```txt
00 ZETTELKASTEN
10 PROJECTS
20 DOCUMENTATION
30 DEEP RESEARCH
40 TECH STACK
50 REGRETS, CIGARETTES, & NEURAL NETS
90 OBSIDIAN
```

## Note Identity Rule

The filename should tell you what the note is without opening it.

Bad:

```txt
Plan.md
Notes.md
Spec.md
New Thing.md
```

Good:

```txt
rateltd.commands.execution.contract.md
vouch.readiness.gates.contract.md
ctrlplus.architecture.boundaries.contract.md
```

## Numbers

Use numbers for folder sorting, not semantic meaning.

Use dot notation for semantic meaning.

## Status Values

```txt
draft
active
review
deprecated
superseded
archive
```

## Authority Values

```txt
source-of-truth
working-note
reference
derived
legacy
archive
```
'@

Write-TextFile "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.contracts.naming-standard.md" $namingStandard

$propertySchema = @'
---
title: Obsidian Contract Property Schema
type: standard
scope: vault
domain: obsidian
artifact: properties
kind: schema
namespace: obsidian.contracts.property-schema
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
tags:
  - obsidian/contracts
  - obsidian/properties
  - status/active
---

# Obsidian Contract Property Schema

## Required Properties for Serious Notes

```yaml
---
title:
type:
scope:
project:
domain:
artifact:
kind:
namespace:
status:
authority:
parent:
depends_on:
supersedes:
tags:
created:
updated:
---
```

## Property Definitions

| Property | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `title` | Human-readable title. |
| `type` | Broad note class: contract, reference, workflow, map, research, writing, execution. |
| `scope` | Authority boundary: vault, project, domain, feature, module, operation, reference. |
| `project` | Owning project when applicable. |
| `domain` | Rule boundary or topic area. |
| `artifact` | Specific subject inside the domain. |
| `kind` | Artifact kind: contract, map, checklist, workflow, spec, report. |
| `namespace` | Dot-notation semantic address. |
| `status` | Lifecycle state. |
| `authority` | Whether this note is source-of-truth, reference, draft, legacy, etc. |
| `parent` | Primary parent note. |
| `depends_on` | Notes this note relies on. |
| `supersedes` | Older notes replaced by this note. |
| `tags` | Search/filter labels. |
| `created` | Creation date. |
| `updated` | Last meaningful update date. |

## Example

```yaml
---
title: RateLtd Commands Registry Contract
type: contract
scope: project
project: RateLtd
domain: commands
artifact: registry
kind: contract
namespace: rateltd.commands.registry.contract
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
parent: "[[rateltd.project.map]]"
depends_on:
  - "[[rateltd.product.vision]]"
supersedes: []
tags:
  - projects/rateltd
  - contracts/commands
  - status/active
created: 2026-05-06
updated: 2026-05-06
---
```
'@

Write-TextFile "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.contracts.property-schema.md" $propertySchema

$amoebaWorkflow = @'
---
title: Obsidian Zettelkasten Amoeba Workflow
type: workflow
scope: vault
domain: obsidian
artifact: zettelkasten
kind: workflow
namespace: obsidian.zettelkasten.amoeba-workflow
status: active
authority: source-of-truth
tags:
  - obsidian/zettelkasten
  - obsidian/workflows
  - status/active
---

# Obsidian Zettelkasten Amoeba Workflow

## Purpose

Turn messy scratch notes into durable atomic notes without killing flow.

## Workflow

1. Capture raw material in `00 ZETTELKASTEN`.
2. Highlight a useful section.
3. Use Note Composer to extract the selection into a new note.
4. Name the note using dot notation.
5. Add minimal properties.
6. Replace the original section with a link.
7. Add the new note to a map or contract when it becomes durable.

## Example

Raw section:

```txt
RateLtd needs one canonical command registry. Commands should be searchable, executable, logged, and tied to PowerShell handlers.
```

Extract to:

```txt
10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/commands/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
```

Original scratch note becomes:

```txt
See [[rateltd.commands.registry.contract]]
```

## Rule

Scratch notes are allowed to be messy.

Contract notes are not.
'@

Write-TextFile "90 OBSIDIAN/Workflows/obsidian.zettelkasten.amoeba-workflow.md" $amoebaWorkflow

## Set a Property

obsidian property:set path="note.md" name="status" value="active" type=text
```

## Search a Namespace

obsidian search query=".contract"
```

# DevNotes Vault Contract-System Bootstrap
# Run from the root of your Obsidian vault.
# This creates structure, moves obvious files, creates templates, and writes operating docs.
# No deletes. No permanent operations.

Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$VaultRoot = (Get-Location).Path

if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
  throw "Run this from the root of your Obsidian vault. I don't see a .obsidian folder here: $VaultRoot"
}

$ObsCmd = Get-Command obsidian -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
if (-not $ObsCmd) {
  throw "Obsidian CLI not found. Install/enable it first, then rerun this."
}

function Ensure-Dir {
  param([Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path)
  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
    Write-Host "DIR  + $Path" -ForegroundColor DarkGreen
  }
}

function Ensure-Parent {
  param([Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path)
  $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent
  if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent }
}

function Move-FolderSafe {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To
  )

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP folder missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    return
  }

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To)) {
    Ensure-Parent $To
    Move-Item -LiteralPath $From -Destination $To
    Write-Host "MOVE folder: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    return
  }

  Write-Host "MERGE folder contents: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow
  Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $From -Force | ForEach-Object {
    $dest = Join-Path $To $_.Name
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dest)) {
      Move-Item -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Destination $dest
      Write-Host "  MOVE child: $($_.Name)" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    } else {
      Write-Host "  SKIP conflict: $dest" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    }
  }
}

function Move-NoteSafe {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To
  )

  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP note missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    return
  }

  if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) {
    Write-Host "SKIP note exists: $To" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    return
  }

  Ensure-Parent $To
  & obsidian move "path=$From" "to=$To" | Out-Host
  Write-Host "MOVE note: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
}

function Write-TextFile {
  param(
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path,
    [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Content,
    [switch]$Overwrite
  )

  Ensure-Parent $Path

  if ((Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path) -and (-not $Overwrite)) {
    Write-Host "SKIP file exists: $Path" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
    return
  }

  $Content | Set-Content -LiteralPath $Path -Encoding UTF8
  Write-Host "WRITE file: $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
}

$Path | Out-Null Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor DarkGreen } } function Ensure-Parent { param([Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path) $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent } } function Move-FolderSafe { param( [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From, [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To ) if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) { Write-Host "SKIP folder missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray return } if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To)) { Ensure-Parent $To Move-Item -LiteralPath $From -Destination $To Write-Host "MOVE folder: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan return } Write-Host "MERGE folder contents: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $From -Force | ForEach-Object { $dest = Join-Path $To $_.Name if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dest)) { Move-Item -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Destination $dest Write-Host " MOVE child: $($_.Name)" -ForegroundColor Cyan } else { Write-Host " SKIP conflict: $dest" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow } } } function Move-NoteSafe { param( [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$From, [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$To ) if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) { Write-Host "SKIP note missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray return } if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) { Write-Host "SKIP note exists: $To" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow return } Ensure-Parent $To & obsidian move "path=$From" "to=$To" | Out-Host Write-Host "MOVE note: $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan } function Write-TextFile { param( [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Path, [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)][string]$Content, [switch]$Overwrite ) Ensure-Parent $Path if ((Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path) -and (-not $Overwrite)) { Write-Host "SKIP file exists: $Path"     Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    function Ensure-Dir {
      param([string]$Path)
      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
        Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
      }
    }

    function Move-FolderSafe {
      param([string]$From, [string]$To)

      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
        Write-Host "SKIP missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
        return
      }

      if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) {
        Write-Host "SKIP exists: $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        return
      }

      Move-Item -LiteralPath $From -Destination $To
      Write-Host "MOVE $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    Write-Host "== TOP LEVEL ==" -ForegroundColor Magenta

Paste this:

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    function Ensure-Dir {
      param([string]$Path)
      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
        Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
      }
    }

    function Ensure-Parent {
      param([string]$Path)
      $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent
      if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent }
    }

    function Move-FileSafe {
      param([string]$From, [string]$To)

      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $From)) {
        Write-Host "SKIP missing: $From" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
        return
      }

      if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) {
        Write-Host "SKIP exists: $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        return
      }

      Ensure-Parent $To
      Move-Item -LiteralPath $From -Destination $To
      Write-Host "MOVE $From -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    Write-Host "== ZETTELKASTEN CAPTURES ==" -ForegroundColor Magenta

    Move-FileSafe "Untitled.md" "00 ZETTELKASTEN/untitled.000.capture.md"

Pass 2 did **no note moves**. It only changed `.obsidian/workspace.json`, then committed that. Not harmful, but the script could not find the source files, so we need a more flexible pass that searches by filename instead of assuming the exact current path. îˆ€fileciteîˆ‚turn7file0îˆ

Use **pass2b**.

Run:

Paste this whole script into Notepad, save, close, then run it.

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    function Ensure-Dir {
      param([string]$Path)
      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
        Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
      }
    }

    function Ensure-Parent {
      param([string]$Path)
      $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent
      if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent }
    }

    function Move-ByName {
      param(
        [string]$Root,
        [string]$Leaf,
        [string]$To
      )

      if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $To) {
        Write-Host "SKIP exists: $To" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        return
      }

      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Root)) {
        Write-Host "SKIP root missing: $Root" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
        return
      }

      $matches = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Root -Recurse -File -Force | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $Leaf })

      if ($matches.Count -eq 0) {
        Write-Host "SKIP not found under $Root :: $Leaf" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
        return
      }

      if ($matches.Count -gt 1) {
        Write-Host "AMBIGUOUS $Leaf under $Root" -ForegroundColor Red
        $matches | ForEach-Object { Write-Host "  $($_.FullName)" -ForegroundColor Red }
        return
      }

      Ensure-Parent $To
      Move-Item -LiteralPath $matches[0].FullName -Destination $To
      Write-Host "MOVE $($matches[0].FullName) -> $To" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    Write-Host "== ZETTELKASTEN ==" -ForegroundColor Magenta

    Move-ByName "." "Untitled.md" "00 ZETTELKASTEN/untitled.000.capture.md"
    Move-ByName "." "Untitled 1.md" "00 ZETTELKASTEN/untitled.001.capture.md"
    Move-ByName "." "Untitled 2.md" "00 ZETTELKASTEN/untitled.002.capture.md"
    Move-ByName "." "File Types for Documentation.md" "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.documentation.file-types.md"

    Write-Host "== RATELTD ==" -ForegroundColor Magenta

    Move-ByName "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd" "00 - RateLtd Index.md" "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/00 PROJECT MAP/rateltd.project.map.md"

    Move-ByName "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd" "03 - Architecture Plan.md" "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/30 ARCHITECTURE/rateltd.architecture.plan.md"
    Move-ByName "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd" "04 - Module Specifications.md" "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/rateltd.modules.specifications.contract.md"
    Move-ByName "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd" "05 - Integration Plan.md" "10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/integrations/rateltd.integrations.plan.contract.md"

    Write-Host "== VOUCH ==" -ForegroundColor Magenta

Good. Youâ€™re clean enough to continue.

`.agent-logs/events.jsonl` is modified because the agent/tool runner keeps writing history. Since you want agents to read that later, keep it tracked and just commit it at the end of each work pass.

Do **Pass 3A** now: foundation docs + dashboards + template folder shell. No moves.

Run:

    notepad .\_OPS\devnotes-bootstrap-pass3a.ps1

Paste this:

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    function Ensure-Dir {
      param([string]$Path)
      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
        Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
      }
    }

    function Write-Note {
      param(
        [string]$Path,
        [string]$Content
      )

      $parent = Split-Path -Path $Path -Parent
      if ($parent) { Ensure-Dir $parent }

      if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path) {
        Write-Host "SKIP exists: $Path" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        return
      }

      $Content | Set-Content -LiteralPath $Path -Encoding UTF8
      Write-Host "WRITE $Path" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    Ensure-Dir "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts"
    Ensure-Dir "90 OBSIDIAN/Workflows"
    Ensure-Dir "90 OBSIDIAN/Dashboards"
    Ensure-Dir "90 OBSIDIAN/OB-Templates/Contracts"

    $naming = @'
    ---
    title: Obsidian Contract Naming Standard
    type: standard
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: naming
    kind: standard
    namespace: obsidian.contracts.naming-standard
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent:
    depends_on: []
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/contracts
      - obsidian/naming
      - status/active
    ---

    # Obsidian Contract Naming Standard

    ## Core Standard

    This vault uses a hybrid knowledge architecture:

    - Numbered all-caps folders for top-level navigation.
    - Dendron-style dot notation for semantic file identity.
    - Zettelkasten extraction for atomic note growth.
    - Contract-style notes for source-of-truth project rules.
    - Obsidian properties for machine-readable metadata.
    - Links for graph relationships.

    ## Naming Formula

        project.domain.artifact.kind.md

    Examples:

        rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md
        rateltd.tui-shell.layout.contract.md
        vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
        vouch.authz.roles.contract.md
        ctrlplus.visualizer.pipeline.contract.md
        obsidian.zettelkasten.amoeba-workflow.md

    ## Folder Rule

    Folders are neighborhoods. File names carry identity.

    Top-level folders:

        00 ZETTELKASTEN
        10 PROJECTS
        20 DOCUMENTATION
        30 DEEP RESEARCH
        40 TECH STACK
        50 REGRETS, CIGARETTES, & NEURAL NETS
        90 OBSIDIAN

    ## File Identity Rule

    A durable note name should answer:

    1. What project or scope owns this?
    2. What domain does it belong to?
    3. What artifact is it describing?
    4. What kind of note is it?

    ## Good Names

        vouch.readiness.gates.contract.md
        vouch.payments.lifecycle.contract.md
        rateltd.commands.execution.contract.md
        ctrlplus.architecture.boundaries.contract.md

    ## Bad Names

        Notes.md
        Plan.md
        New Spec.md
        Random Thoughts.md

    ## Numbers

    Use numbers for folder ordering only.

    Use dot notation for semantic identity.

    ## Standard Kinds

    - contract
    - map
    - workflow
    - reference
    - research
    - checklist
    - work-package
    - handoff
    - standard
    - dashboard
    - legacy

    ## Standard Statuses

    - draft
    - active
    - review
    - deprecated
    - superseded
    - archived

    ## Standard Authority Levels

    - source-of-truth
    - working-note
    - reference
    - derived
    - legacy
    - archive
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.contracts.naming-standard.md" $naming

    $properties = @'
    ---
    title: Obsidian Contract Property Schema
    type: standard
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: properties
    kind: schema
    namespace: obsidian.contracts.property-schema
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent: "[[obsidian.contracts.naming-standard]]"
    depends_on:
      - "[[obsidian.contracts.naming-standard]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/contracts
      - obsidian/properties
      - status/active
    ---

    # Obsidian Contract Property Schema

    ## Required Properties for Durable Notes

        title:
        type:
        scope:
        project:
        domain:
        artifact:
        kind:
        namespace:
        status:
        authority:
        parent:
        depends_on:
        supersedes:
        tags:

    ## Property Meanings

    | Property | Meaning |
    |---|---|
    | title | Human-readable title. |
    | type | Broad note class: contract, map, workflow, reference, research, writing, execution. |
    | scope | Boundary: vault, project, domain, feature, module, operation, reference. |
    | project | Owning project when applicable. |
    | domain | Rule boundary or topic area. |
    | artifact | Specific thing inside the domain. |
    | kind | Specific note kind. |
    | namespace | Dot-notation semantic address. |
    | status | Lifecycle state. |
    | authority | Whether the note is source-of-truth, reference, draft, legacy, etc. |
    | parent | Primary parent note or map. |
    | depends_on | Notes this note relies on. |
    | supersedes | Older notes replaced by this note. |
    | tags | Search and filter labels. |

    ## Example

        ---
        title: RateLtd Commands Registry Contract
        type: contract
        scope: project
        project: RateLtd
        domain: commands
        artifact: registry
        kind: contract
        namespace: rateltd.commands.registry.contract
        status: active
        authority: source-of-truth
        parent: "[[rateltd.project.map]]"
        depends_on:
          - "[[rateltd.product.vision]]"
        supersedes: []
        tags:
          - projects/rateltd
          - contracts/commands
          - status/active
        ---

    ## Rule

    Every serious note should be findable by:

    - folder
    - namespace
    - project
    - domain
    - kind
    - status
    - authority
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.contracts.property-schema.md" $properties

    $noteTypes = @'
    ---
    title: Obsidian Contract Note Types
    type: standard
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: note-types
    kind: standard
    namespace: obsidian.contracts.note-types
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent: "[[obsidian.contracts.naming-standard]]"
    depends_on:
      - "[[obsidian.contracts.property-schema]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/contracts
      - obsidian/note-types
      - status/active
    ---

    # Obsidian Contract Note Types

    ## Contract

    A source-of-truth rule document. Contracts define what must be true, what must not happen, and how work is judged.

    ## Map

    A navigation note. Maps gather links, dashboards, and summaries for a project, domain, or workflow.

    ## Workflow

    A repeatable operating procedure.

    ## Reference

    Stable supporting knowledge. Reference notes explain tools, APIs, libraries, standards, or concepts.

    ## Research

    Exploratory or investigative material. Research notes may become contracts later.

    ## Work Package

    A bounded implementation job for Codex, ChatGPT, or a human operator.

    ## Handoff

    A state-transfer note that lets the next operator resume work without re-discovery.

    ## Dashboard

    A Dataview-powered operating surface.

    ## Legacy

    A preserved note that may still contain useful information but is no longer the active source of truth.

    ## Rule

    Do not force every note to become a contract.

    Contracts are for durable truth.
    Zettelkasten notes are for growth.
    Research notes are for discovery.
    Work packages are for execution.
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Contracts/obsidian.contracts.note-types.md" $noteTypes

    $workflow = @'
    ---
    title: Obsidian Zettelkasten Amoeba Workflow
    type: workflow
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: zettelkasten
    kind: workflow
    namespace: obsidian.zettelkasten.amoeba-workflow
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent:
    depends_on:
      - "[[obsidian.contracts.naming-standard]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/zettelkasten
      - obsidian/workflows
      - status/active
    ---

    # Obsidian Zettelkasten Amoeba Workflow

    ## Purpose

    Turn messy scratch notes into durable atomic notes without killing flow.

    ## Capture

    Raw thoughts go in:

        00 ZETTELKASTEN

    Capture notes may be messy. They are not required to follow contract structure.

    ## Extract

    When a section becomes useful:

    1. Highlight the useful section.
    2. Use Note Composer or Note Refactor to extract it.
    3. Give the new note a semantic dot-name.
    4. Add basic properties.
    5. Replace the source section with a link.

    ## Example

    Source idea:

        RateLtd needs one canonical command registry. Commands should be searchable, executable, logged, and tied to PowerShell handlers.

    Extracted note:

        10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/commands/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md

    Source note becomes:

        See [[rateltd.commands.registry.contract]]

    ## Rule

    Scratch notes are allowed to be messy.

    Contract notes are not.
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Workflows/obsidian.zettelkasten.amoeba-workflow.md" $workflow

    $cli = @'
    ---
    title: Obsidian CLI Workflow
    type: workflow
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: cli
    kind: workflow
    namespace: obsidian.cli.workflow
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent:
    depends_on:
      - "[[obsidian.contracts.naming-standard]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/cli
      - obsidian/workflows
      - status/active
    ---

    # Obsidian CLI Workflow

    ## Common Inventory Commands

        obsidian files
        obsidian folders
        obsidian folders total
        obsidian files total
        obsidian tags counts sort=count
        obsidian properties counts sort=count
        obsidian orphans total
        obsidian deadends total
        obsidian unresolved counts

    ## Search

        obsidian search query="rateltd.commands"
        obsidian search query=".contract"
        obsidian search query="vouch.payments"

    ## Read

        obsidian read path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/00 PROJECT MAP/rateltd.project.map.md"

    ## Move

        obsidian move path="old/path.md" to="new/path.md"

    ## Properties

        obsidian property:set path="note.md" name="status" value="active" type=text
        obsidian property:set path="note.md" name="type" value="contract" type=text
        obsidian property:read path="note.md" name="namespace"

    ## Daily Operating Loop

    1. Capture quickly.
    2. Extract good sections.
    3. Promote durable rules into contracts.
    4. Link contracts to project maps.
    5. Use Dataview dashboards to inspect the system.
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Workflows/obsidian.cli.workflow.md" $cli

    $dashboard = @'
    ---
    title: Contract Dashboard
    type: dashboard
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: obsidian
    artifact: contracts
    kind: dashboard
    namespace: obsidian.dashboard.contracts
    status: active
    authority: reference
    parent:
    depends_on:
      - "[[obsidian.contracts.property-schema]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - obsidian/dashboard
      - contracts
      - status/active
    ---

    # Contract Dashboard

    ## Active Contracts

        TABLE project, domain, artifact, kind, status, authority
        FROM "10 PROJECTS" OR "90 OBSIDIAN"
        WHERE type = "contract" AND status = "active"
        SORT project ASC, domain ASC, artifact ASC

    ## Draft Contracts

        TABLE project, domain, artifact, kind, updated
        FROM "10 PROJECTS" OR "90 OBSIDIAN"
        WHERE type = "contract" AND status = "draft"
        SORT updated DESC

    ## Source-of-Truth Notes

        TABLE type, project, domain, artifact, status
        FROM ""
        WHERE authority = "source-of-truth"
        SORT project ASC, domain ASC

    ## Work Packages

        TABLE project, domain, status, updated
        FROM "10 PROJECTS"
        WHERE kind = "work-package"
        SORT updated DESC

    ## Note

    Convert the indented Dataview queries into Dataview code blocks inside Obsidian if needed.
    '@

    Write-Note "90 OBSIDIAN/Dashboards/obsidian.dashboard.contracts.md" $dashboard

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "PASS 3A COMPLETE" -ForegroundColor Green
