### assistant 2026-05-07T03:56:58.672Z

Good. Pass 3A landed clean.

Now do **Pass 3B: contract templates only**.

Run:

    notepad .\_OPS\devnotes-bootstrap-pass3b.ps1

Paste this script, save, close Notepad, then run the commands below it.

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    function Ensure-Dir {
      param([string]$Path)
      if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $Path | Out-Null
        Write-Host "DIR + $Path" -ForegroundColor Green
      }
    }

    function Write-Template {
      param(
        [string]$FileName,
        [string]$Title,
        [string]$Kind,
        [string]$Purpose,
        [string]$UseWhen,
        [string[]]$Sections,
        [string[]]$Checks
      )

      $dir = "90 OBSIDIAN/OB-Templates/Contracts"
      Ensure-Dir $dir
      $path = Join-Path $dir $FileName

      if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $path) {
        Write-Host "SKIP exists: $path" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        return
      }

      $sectionText = ($Sections | ForEach-Object { "## $_`n`n- `n" }) -join "`n"
      $checkText = ($Checks | ForEach-Object { "- [ ] $_" }) -join "`n"

      $content = @"
    ---
    title: "<% tp.file.title %>"
    type: contract
    scope: project
    project:
    domain:
    artifact:
    kind: $Kind
    namespace:
    status: draft
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent:
    depends_on: []
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - contracts/$Kind
      - status/draft
    created: <% tp.date.now("YYYY-MM-DD") %>
    updated: <% tp.date.now("YYYY-MM-DD") %>
    ---

    # <% tp.file.title %>

    ## Contract Kind

    $Kind

    ## Purpose

    $Purpose

    ## Use This Template When

    $UseWhen

    ## Authority

    This document is a contract. It defines source-of-truth rules that implementation, prompts, tests, and future documentation must obey until this contract is superseded.

    ## Scope

    ### Governs

    - 
    - 
    - 

    ### Does Not Govern

    - 
    - 
    - 

    ## Non-Negotiable Rules

    1. 
    2. 
    3. 

    ## Required Invariants

    - 
    - 
    - 

    $sectionText

    ## Dependencies

    | Dependency | Type | Reason |
    |---|---|---|
    |  |  |  |

    ## Implementation References

    | Area | Path / Link | Notes |
    |---|---|---|
    | Routes |  |  |
    | Actions |  |  |
    | Fetchers |  |  |
    | Components |  |  |
    | Schemas |  |  |
    | Types |  |  |
    | Data |  |  |
    | Tests |  |  |

    ## Failure Modes

    | Failure | Cause | Required Handling |
    |---|---|---|
    |  |  |  |

    ## Acceptance Criteria

    $checkText

    ## Verification Checklist

    - [ ] Namespace is correct.
    - [ ] Scope is explicit.
    - [ ] Domain boundary is clear.
    - [ ] Rules are testable.
    - [ ] Forbidden behavior is documented.
    - [ ] Dependencies are linked.
    - [ ] Implementation references are listed.
    - [ ] This contract does not duplicate another active source-of-truth note.

    ## Open Questions

    - 

    ## Changelog

    | Date | Change | Reason |
    |---|---|---|
    | <% tp.date.now("YYYY-MM-DD") %> | Created | Initial draft |
    "@

      $content | Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Encoding UTF8
      Write-Host "WRITE $path" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    $templates = @(
      @{
        FileName = "product-definition.contract.md"
        Title = "Product Definition Contract"
        Kind = "product-definition"
        Purpose = "Defines what the product is, who it serves, what it refuses to be, and what success means."
        UseWhen = "You need a durable source of truth for the product identity, positioning, scope, user promise, and non-goals."
        Sections = @("Product Definition","Target Users","Core Promise","Non-Goals","Success Conditions","Positioning Boundaries")
        Checks = @("Product identity is clear.","Target users are defined.","Non-goals prevent scope drift.","Success can be evaluated.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "domain-model.contract.md"
        Title = "Domain Model Contract"
        Kind = "domain-model"
        Purpose = "Defines the canonical nouns, entities, relationships, invariants, and vocabulary of a domain."
        UseWhen = "The same nouns keep appearing across code, docs, prompts, database models, routes, and UI states."
        Sections = @("Domain Vocabulary","Entities","Relationships","Invariants","Forbidden Interpretations","Glossary")
        Checks = @("Domain nouns are canonical.","Entity relationships are explicit.","Invariants are documented.","Ambiguous terms are resolved.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "state-machine.contract.md"
        Title = "State Machine Contract"
        Kind = "state-machine"
        Purpose = "Defines allowed states, transitions, guards, terminal states, invalid transitions, and reconciliation behavior."
        UseWhen = "A workflow can move between states and invalid transitions would create bugs, money risk, trust risk, or data corruption."
        Sections = @("States","Initial State","Terminal States","Transitions","Transition Guards","Invalid Transitions","Reconciliation Rules")
        Checks = @("Every state is defined.","Every transition has from/to states.","Guards are explicit.","Invalid transitions are named.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "lifecycle.contract.md"
        Title = "Lifecycle Contract"
        Kind = "lifecycle"
        Purpose = "Defines the end-to-end lifecycle of a domain object, workflow, user journey, or operational process."
        UseWhen = "You need to describe what happens from creation to completion, expiration, archive, or failure."
        Sections = @("Lifecycle Overview","Creation","Active Phase","Mutation Rules","Completion","Expiration","Archive Rules")
        Checks = @("Lifecycle start is defined.","Lifecycle end states are defined.","Mutation rules are explicit.","Failure paths are included.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "roles.contract.md"
        Title = "Roles Contract"
        Kind = "roles"
        Purpose = "Defines actors, responsibilities, capabilities, forbidden actions, and role-specific expectations."
        UseWhen = "Multiple actors use the system and each actor needs a clear responsibility and permission boundary."
        Sections = @("Actors","Role Responsibilities","Role Capabilities","Forbidden Actions","Role Conflicts","Role-Specific UI Rules")
        Checks = @("Each role has a clear purpose.","Allowed actions are listed.","Forbidden actions are listed.","Role conflicts are handled.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "authz.contract.md"
        Title = "AuthZ Contract"
        Kind = "authz"
        Purpose = "Defines permission boundaries, guards, access rules, ownership checks, and authorization verification."
        UseWhen = "A route, action, fetcher, object, or workflow needs explicit rules for who can do what and when."
        Sections = @("Authorization Model","Access Rules","Ownership Rules","Route Guards","Action Guards","Fetcher Guards","Test Cases")
        Checks = @("Access rules are explicit.","Ownership checks are documented.","Server-side enforcement is required.","Bypass paths are named.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "readiness-gates.contract.md"
        Title = "Readiness Gates Contract"
        Kind = "readiness-gates"
        Purpose = "Defines prerequisites that must be satisfied before a user, account, object, or workflow can proceed."
        UseWhen = "The system must block, warn, or redirect based on setup state, verification state, or provider readiness."
        Sections = @("Gate Overview","Required Gates","Blocking Conditions","Warning Conditions","Bypass Rules","UI Messaging","Recheck Rules")
        Checks = @("All gates are named.","Blocking behavior is clear.","User-facing messaging is defined.","Recheck behavior is specified.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "acceptance-gates.contract.md"
        Title = "Acceptance Gates Contract"
        Kind = "acceptance-gates"
        Purpose = "Defines objective completion criteria for features, workflows, releases, migrations, or agent work packages."
        UseWhen = "You need to prevent vague completion claims and require verifiable evidence before work is accepted."
        Sections = @("Acceptance Summary","Functional Gates","Data Gates","UI Gates","Security Gates","Test Gates","Release Gates")
        Checks = @("Acceptance gates are measurable.","Required evidence is listed.","Failure means not done.","Release blockers are explicit.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "feature-inventory.contract.md"
        Title = "Feature Inventory Contract"
        Kind = "feature-inventory"
        Purpose = "Defines the canonical list of features, status, ownership, dependencies, and required behavior."
        UseWhen = "A project has multiple features and you need one source of truth for scope, status, and dependencies."
        Sections = @("Feature Catalog","Feature Status","Required Features","Deferred Features","Dependencies","Completion Rules","Cut Lines")
        Checks = @("Every feature has a status.","Required vs deferred is clear.","Dependencies are documented.","Scope cut lines exist.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "route-map.contract.md"
        Title = "Route Map Contract"
        Kind = "route-map"
        Purpose = "Defines application routes, ownership, page intent, auth requirements, params, search params, and data dependencies."
        UseWhen = "Routes need to remain thin, intentional, protected, and aligned with feature/domain ownership."
        Sections = @("Route Catalog","Route Ownership","Auth Requirements","Params","Search Params","Data Dependencies","Redirect Rules")
        Checks = @("Each route has an owner.","Auth behavior is documented.","Params are named.","Data dependencies are listed.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "module-spec.contract.md"
        Title = "Module Spec Contract"
        Kind = "module-spec"
        Purpose = "Defines module boundaries, responsibilities, imports, exports, dependency rules, and forbidden coupling."
        UseWhen = "You need to keep a codebase from drifting into tangled imports, mixed responsibilities, or unclear ownership."
        Sections = @("Module Purpose","Owned Responsibilities","Public Interface","Allowed Imports","Forbidden Imports","Data Flow","Boundary Tests")
        Checks = @("Module responsibility is clear.","Allowed imports are defined.","Forbidden imports are defined.","Public API is documented.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "data-model.contract.md"
        Title = "Data Model Contract"
        Kind = "data-model"
        Purpose = "Defines entities, fields, relationships, enums, indexes, constraints, DTO mappings, and persistence rules."
        UseWhen = "Database shape, transport shape, and business rules need to agree before implementation."
        Sections = @("Entities","Fields","Enums","Relationships","Indexes","Constraints","DTO Mapping","Migration Notes")
        Checks = @("Entities are listed.","Critical fields are defined.","Relationships are explicit.","DTO mapping is included.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "api-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "API Contract"
        Kind = "api-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines endpoints, request contracts, response contracts, errors, auth, idempotency, and integration behavior."
        UseWhen = "A system boundary exposes or consumes HTTP APIs, server actions, RPC-like operations, or provider calls."
        Sections = @("Endpoint Catalog","Requests","Responses","Errors","Auth","Idempotency","Rate Limits","Examples")
        Checks = @("Requests are defined.","Responses are defined.","Errors are defined.","Auth requirements are explicit.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "event-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "Event Contract"
        Kind = "event-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines events, producers, consumers, payloads, ordering expectations, idempotency, replay, and failure handling."
        UseWhen = "State changes are emitted, consumed, audited, replayed, or used to trigger downstream workflows."
        Sections = @("Event Catalog","Producers","Consumers","Payloads","Ordering","Idempotency","Replay Rules","Failure Handling")
        Checks = @("Events are named.","Payloads are defined.","Consumers are listed.","Idempotency is addressed.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "webhook-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "Webhook Contract"
        Kind = "webhook-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines inbound and outbound webhook events, verification, retry behavior, reconciliation, and failure recovery."
        UseWhen = "External providers or internal services send asynchronous event notifications."
        Sections = @("Webhook Catalog","Verification","Payloads","Retry Rules","Idempotency","Reconciliation","Failure Handling","Security")
        Checks = @("Webhook verification is required.","Retry behavior is clear.","Idempotency is defined.","Reconciliation rules exist.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "integration-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "Integration Contract"
        Kind = "integration-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines provider responsibilities, credentials, data mapping, flows, failures, reconciliation, and operational boundaries."
        UseWhen = "The project depends on an external service like Stripe, Clerk, Cloudinary, Hugging Face, Vercel, or GitHub."
        Sections = @("Provider Role","Credential Model","User Flow","Data Mapping","Failure Modes","Reconciliation","Operational Limits","Security")
        Checks = @("Provider role is clear.","Credential handling is defined.","Failure modes are listed.","Reconciliation is specified.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "ui-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "UI Contract"
        Kind = "ui-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines visible states, user interactions, empty/loading/error states, responsive behavior, and accessibility expectations."
        UseWhen = "A page, component, or flow needs consistent visual and behavioral rules."
        Sections = @("UI Purpose","Visible States","Interactions","Empty State","Loading State","Error State","Responsive Behavior","Accessibility")
        Checks = @("Visible states are listed.","Interaction behavior is clear.","Error states are defined.","Accessibility expectations exist.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "copy-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "Copy Contract"
        Kind = "copy-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines product language, labels, messages, forbidden claims, legal boundaries, and terminology consistency."
        UseWhen = "Words matter for trust, compliance, conversion, onboarding, or product positioning."
        Sections = @("Voice Rules","Approved Terms","Forbidden Terms","UI Copy","Legal-Sensitive Copy","Error Messages","Empty States","Examples")
        Checks = @("Approved language is listed.","Forbidden claims are listed.","Legal-sensitive copy is marked.","Examples are included.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "test-contract.contract.md"
        Title = "Test Contract"
        Kind = "test-contract"
        Purpose = "Defines test coverage requirements, regression checks, fixtures, mocks, acceptance tests, and verification workflow."
        UseWhen = "A feature or domain must be protected by repeatable tests before it can be considered shippable."
        Sections = @("Coverage Requirements","Unit Tests","Integration Tests","E2E Tests","Fixtures","Regression Cases","Manual QA","Release Verification")
        Checks = @("Coverage requirements are explicit.","Regression cases are listed.","Manual QA exists where needed.","Release verification is concrete.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "codex-rules.contract.md"
        Title = "Codex Rules Contract"
        Kind = "codex-rules"
        Purpose = "Defines agent execution rules, boundaries, stop conditions, verification requirements, and forbidden shortcuts."
        UseWhen = "Codex or another agent will modify code, docs, prompts, schemas, workflows, or repo structure."
        Sections = @("Execution Rules","Allowed Changes","Forbidden Changes","Boundary Rules","Stop Conditions","Verification Requirements","Reporting Format","Failure Handling")
        Checks = @("Allowed work is explicit.","Forbidden work is explicit.","Stop conditions exist.","Verification is mandatory.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "work-package.contract.md"
        Title = "Work Package Contract"
        Kind = "work-package"
        Purpose = "Defines a bounded implementation job with objective scope, inputs, outputs, constraints, and completion evidence."
        UseWhen = "You need to hand a precise job to Codex, ChatGPT, or future-you without losing intent."
        Sections = @("Objective","Context","Inputs","Expected Outputs","Constraints","Step Plan","Verification","Completion Report")
        Checks = @("Objective is bounded.","Inputs are listed.","Outputs are concrete.","Completion evidence is required.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "verification-checklist.contract.md"
        Title = "Verification Checklist Contract"
        Kind = "verification-checklist"
        Purpose = "Defines concrete checks required before accepting a change, release, refactor, migration, or generated output."
        UseWhen = "You need a final gate that prevents wishful thinking, incomplete execution, or silent drift."
        Sections = @("Verification Scope","Functional Checks","Architecture Checks","Security Checks","Data Checks","UI Checks","Test Checks","Release Checks")
        Checks = @("Checks are concrete.","Each check can pass or fail.","Critical blockers are named.","Evidence requirements are included.")
      },
      @{
        FileName = "handoff.contract.md"
        Title = "Handoff Contract"
        Kind = "handoff"
        Purpose = "Defines current state, completed work, remaining work, blockers, risks, assumptions, and next operator instructions."
        UseWhen = "Work is being paused, transferred, resumed later, or handed from one agent/operator/session to another."
        Sections = @("Current State","Completed Work","Remaining Work","Known Blockers","Risks","Assumptions","Next Actions","Recovery Instructions")
        Checks = @("Current state is clear.","Completed work is listed.","Remaining work is actionable.","Next actions are explicit.")
      }
    )

    foreach ($template in $templates) {
      Write-Template `
        -FileName $template.FileName `
        -Title $template.Title `
        -Kind $template.Kind `
        -Purpose $template.Purpose `
        -UseWhen $template.UseWhen `
        -Sections $template.Sections `
        -Checks $template.Checks
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "PASS 3B COMPLETE" -ForegroundColor Green
    git status --short

Run:

    pwsh -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\_OPS\devnotes-bootstrap-pass3b.ps1
    git add -A
    git status --short
    git commit -m "add professional contract templates"

### assistant 2026-05-07T04:24:26.929Z

Good. That was a **partial success**, not a disaster.

The error was simple:

    $home

PowerShell treats `$HOME` / `$home` as the same variable, and `$HOME` is read-only. So the script failed only when trying to create `devnotes.home.md`.

Everything before that wrote successfully and you committed it.

Now run this tiny repair pass.

    notepad .\_OPS\devnotes-bootstrap-pass3c-fix.ps1

Paste this:

    Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    if (-not (Test-Path ".obsidian")) {
      throw "Run this from the DevNotes vault root."
    }

    $homeNote = @'
    ---
    title: DevNotes Home
    type: map
    scope: vault
    project:
    domain: vault
    artifact: home
    kind: map
    namespace: devnotes.home
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth
    parent:
    depends_on:
      - "[[devnotes.projects.map]]"
      - "[[obsidian.system.map]]"
      - "[[obsidian.dashboard.contracts]]"
    supersedes: []
    tags:
      - devnotes
      - maps/vault
      - status/active
    ---

    # DevNotes Home

    ## Primary Maps

    - [[devnotes.projects.map]]
    - [[devnotes.zettelkasten.map]]
    - [[devnotes.documentation.map]]
    - [[devnotes.deep-research.map]]
    - [[devnotes.tech-stack.map]]
    - [[devnotes.regrets-cigarettes-neural-nets.map]]
    - [[obsidian.system.map]]
    - [[obsidian.dashboard.contracts]]

    ## Active Source-of-Truth Notes

    ~~~dataview
    TABLE type, project, domain, artifact, status
    FROM ""
    WHERE authority = "source-of-truth"
    SORT project ASC, domain ASC, artifact ASC
    ~~~

    ## Recently Modified

    ~~~dataview
    TABLE file.folder AS Folder, file.mtime AS Modified
    FROM ""
    SORT file.mtime DESC
    LIMIT 25
    ~~~

    ## Contract Templates

    ~~~dataview
    LIST
    FROM "90 OBSIDIAN/OB-Templates/Contracts"
    SORT file.name ASC
    ~~~

    ## Operating Rule

    Capture messy notes quickly. Promote durable rules into contracts. Keep project truth discoverable through maps.
    '@

    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath "devnotes.home.md") {
      Write-Host "SKIP exists: devnotes.home.md" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    } else {
      $homeNote | Set-Content -LiteralPath "devnotes.home.md" -Encoding UTF8
      Write-Host "WRITE devnotes.home.md" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "PASS 3C FIX COMPLETE" -ForegroundColor Green
    git status --short

Run:

    pwsh -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\_OPS\devnotes-bootstrap-pass3c-fix.ps1
    git add -A
    git commit -m "add devnotes home map"

After that, run:

    obsidian open path="devnotes.home.md"

Then youâ€™ll have the vault homepage.

System.Collections.Generic.List[object]\n$changed = 0\n$unchanged = 0\n\nforeach ($file in $files) {\n  $rel = Get-RelPath $file.FullName\n  $text = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $file.FullName\n  $meta = Get-MetaForFile $rel $text $file\n  $newText = Add-OrCompleteFrontmatter $text $meta\n  $willChange = ($newText -ne $text)\n\n  if ($willChange) {\n    $changed++\n\n    if ($Apply) {\n      $newText | Set-Content -LiteralPath $file.FullName -Encoding UTF8\n      Write-Host \"NORMALIZE $rel\" -ForegroundColor Cyan\n    } else {\n      Write-Host \"WOULD NORMALIZE $rel\" -ForegroundColor Yellow\n    }\n  } else {\n    $unchanged++\n  }\n\n  $report.Add([pscustomobject]@{\n    path = $rel\n    title = $meta.title\n    type = $meta.type\n    scope = $meta.scope\n    project = $meta.project\n    domain = $meta.domain\n    artifact = $meta.artifact\n    kind = $meta.kind\n    namespace = $meta.namespace\n    status = $meta.status\n    authority = $meta.authority\n    parent = $meta.parent\n    changed = $willChange\n  })\n}\n\n$report | Export-Csv -NoTypeInformation -Encoding UTF8 -Path $ReportPath\n\nWrite-Host \"\"\nWrite-Host \"PROPERTY NORMALIZATION PASS 1: $RunMode\" -ForegroundColor Green\nWrite-Host \"Files scanned: $($files.Count)\"\nWrite-Host \"Changed: $changed\"\nWrite-Host \"Unchanged: $unchanged\"\nWrite-Host \"Report: $ReportPath\"\nWrite-Host \"\"\ngit status --short\n"
    }
  ]

---

Yes. The model and the TUI should share a protocol.

RateLtd is not just a cockpit. It is a **local context protocol surface**.

The clean framing:

RateLtd already knows the kinds of local truth ChatGPT needs:

* current target
* file tree
* selected files
* relevant file contents
* package scripts
* Git status
* diffs
* command output
* errors
* logs
* settings
* recent run/session history

ChatGPT already knows what it can ask RateLtd for:

* â€œgive me repo summaryâ€
* â€œgive me diffâ€
* â€œrun verifyâ€
* â€œapply this file payloadâ€
* â€œopen/preview fileâ€
* â€œexport handoff packetâ€
* â€œshow latest command failureâ€
* â€œcopy sanitized session contextâ€

And RateLtd returns that in a predictable format ChatGPT can consume.

That is the missing product concept:

**RateLtd is the shared interface contract between ChatGPT and your local machine, with you as the authorized operator.**

You stay in the loop, but you stop being the harness.

Your job becomes:

* approve
* trigger
* observe
* paste the handoff back
* decide when to escalate to Codex

The TUIâ€™s job becomes:

* gather local truth
* run safe macros
* apply generated payloads
* capture results
* format outputs for the model
* prevent sloppy human copy/paste errors

So the canonical doctrine is:

**Every RateLtd feature exists because ChatGPT needs either context, execution, mutation, or feedback from the local repo.**

That gives us the real feature taxonomy:

1. **Context providers**
   LauncherLtd, EditorLtd, DifferLtd, LoggerLtd.

2. **Execution providers**
   CommanderLtd Ops, PowerShell macros, package scripts, Git macros.

3. **Mutation providers**
   Clipboard file writes, Git stage/restore, settings writes.

4. **Feedback providers**
   LoggerLtd output, sessions, diagnostics, export handoff.

5. **Safety gates**
   confirmation, redaction, path guards, risk labels, dry preview.

This is the sentence Codex needs burned into its skull:

**Do not build features for Ivan to manually operate a dev tool; build protocol surfaces that let Ivan safely mediate between ChatGPT and the local codebase.**

---

Yes. That locks it in.

RateLtd is a **single-window local operations console** for ChatGPT-assisted coding.

It does not replace:

```txt
GitHub
VS Code
PowerShell
Git
pnpm
File Explorer
Codex
ChatGPT
```

It **wraps the useful parts** so you do not have to context-switch across all of them.

The real purpose:

```txt
One place.
One target.
One set of known actions.
One captured output stream.
One handoff format back to ChatGPT.
```

So the product doctrine becomes:

**RateLtd centralizes the local actions ChatGPT needs you to perform, packages them as safe keyboard macros, captures the result, and formats the result back into model-usable context.**

That means â€œidiot-proofâ€ is not an insult here. It is a design requirement.

Better word: **operator-proof**.

The TUI should assume:

```txt
the human is tired
the model needs precision
manual copying is error-prone
window switching causes mistakes
rate limits punish wasted loops
```

Therefore:

```txt
every action should be visible
every command should be prebuilt
every risky action should confirm
every output should be captured
every handoff should be formatted
every unavailable action should explain itself
```

The final mental model:

**You are not building a dev tool. You are building a local control panel for the human-in-the-loop portion of AI coding.**

That is the source of truth.

---

inference-constrained product orchestration.

Most engineering assumes labor is abundant and compute is cheap enough to treat iteration casually. That assumption breaks under agentic development when implementation power is rate-limited. Suddenly, inference becomes a scarce production resource. Tokens become capital. Context becomes bandwidth. Model turns become execution windows. Under those constraints, the limiting factor is no longer imagination, nor even technical possibility. The limiting factor becomes allocation efficiency. Where should scarce inference be spent to create the greatest realized product value?

That is the problem we are solving.

We are not simply planning software. We are optimizing the conversion of limited model execution into durable product state. Put another way, we are trying to maximize realized implementation per unit of scarce inference. That means reducing ambiguity before prompts are sent, tightening scope to visible truth, eliminating speculative architecture, defining canonical contracts in advance, auditing actual state against intended state, and sending Codex only the highest-leverage delta. Every one of those steps increases output quality while decreasing wasted execution.

This is essentially capital allocation, except the capital is inference.

You can think of it as token economics for product execution, or inference yield optimization, or rate-limit aware systems design. The cleanest phrase, though, is probably inference efficiency engineering.

The purpose is simple: convert scarce model turns into maximum meaningful progress.

Not motion. Progress.

Not lines of code. Product truth.

Not complexity. Alignment.

Thatâ€™s what this process is. It is the disciplined orchestration of scarce inference toward the highest-value implementation outcome.

And thatâ€™s why what weâ€™re doing matters before Codex writes another line. We are increasing the yield of every future turn.

Thatâ€™s a real lever.

---

protocol completeness.

The old instinct in software is feature accumulation. People think progress means adding more capabilities, more settings, more integrations, more surfaces, more buttons, more complexity. On paper, that looks like growth. In reality, it often becomes vanity. The product gets heavier, the architecture sprawls, maintenance increases, attention fragments, and a large percentage of what gets built either goes unused or delivers marginal value. In an agentic coding environment, that vanity becomes especially expensive because every unnecessary abstraction, speculative service, and premature capability consumes inference, context window, implementation effort, and rate-limited execution cycles. In other words, feature chasing burns scarce intelligence on work that does not materially improve the system.

Protocol completeness is a different philosophy.

Instead of asking, â€œWhat else can this product do,â€ it asks, â€œWhat interaction contract must be fully supported for this product to fulfill its purpose?â€ That question is tighter, sharper, and more disciplined. A protocol is the complete loop of meaningful interaction between systems. In your case, the systems are model intelligence, local machine reality, and human supervision. The protocol is how those systems exchange context, execution, mutation, and feedback. If that loop is complete, reliable, and deterministic, the product is complete in the only way that matters, even if it contains relatively few visible features.

Thatâ€™s the elegance of protocol completeness: it is not minimalism for its own sake, and it is not anti-infrastructure. It simply demands justification. Every piece of infrastructure must directly support the protocol. Every visible control must have a real behavioral contract. Every output must feed the loop. Every abstraction must earn its place by making the interaction more complete, more reliable, or more efficient. Anything else is ornamental complexity.

A protocol-complete system is not defined by breadth. It is defined by closure. The loop is whole. Nothing necessary is missing. Nothing unnecessary is consuming attention. It is complete because its contract is complete.

### assistant 2026-05-07T19:38:25.451Z

For **this exact task**, do **not** use Templater, Bases, Advanced Tables, Calendar, or Tasks.

You already have fully formed notes with frontmatter. This is not a â€œcreate from templateâ€ job. This is a **promotion job**.

The proper workflow is:

    capture â†’ triage â†’ promote â†’ link back â†’ commit

Professional vernacular:

    capture note = inbox / raw staging area
    promotion = moving valuable content from scratch into a durable note
    canonical note = final source-of-truth note
    linkback = replacing extracted content with a link to the canonical note
    frontmatter = YAML properties block at the top of the note
    namespace = semantic dot-name identity
    MOC/map = navigation/index note

## Best plugin choice for this task

Use **manual new note creation + paste** for these four notes.

Reason: each target note needs YAML frontmatter at byte zero. Extraction plugins can accidentally leave `## Note 1`, `Vault path:`, or separators above the frontmatter, which would break clean Obsidian properties.

So for this task:

    Best: manual create note + paste exact payload
    Good later: Note Refactor
    Not for this: Templater
    Not for this: Text Transporter
    Not for this: Dataview/Base
    Not for this: Advanced Tables

## Exact task procedure

### 1. Switch the scratch note to source/edit mode

You need to see raw markdown, not rendered preview.

In Obsidian:

    Ctrl+E

or use the reading/edit toggle.

### 2. Create the first target note

In the file explorer, go to:

    10 PROJECTS
    RateLtd
    20 CONTRACTS
    product

Create a new note named:

    rateltd.product.local-context-protocol.contract

Obsidian will add `.md`.

### 3. Paste only the note body

From your scratch note, copy starting at the YAML delimiter:

    ---

through the end of the first noteâ€™s changelog.

Do **not** include:

    ## Note 1
    Vault path:
    horizontal separator before the frontmatter

The target note must start exactly with:

    ---

If anything appears above the opening `---`, the note is dirty.

### 4. Replace the promoted block in the scratch note

Back in `untitled.003.capture`, replace the whole Note 1 block with:

    Promoted to [[rateltd.product.local-context-protocol.contract]]

That is the linkback. It preserves the capture trail without duplicating source-of-truth content.

### 5. Repeat for the other three notes

Create these files:

    10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/product/rateltd.product.local-operations-console.contract.md

    10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/50 AGENT OPS/rateltd.agent-ops.inference-efficiency-engineering.standard.md

    10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/product/rateltd.product.protocol-completeness.contract.md

Replace each section in the scratch note with:

    Promoted to [[rateltd.product.local-operations-console.contract]]

    Promoted to [[rateltd.agent-ops.inference-efficiency-engineering.standard]]

    Promoted to [[rateltd.product.protocol-completeness.contract]]

## Plugin doctrine

### Use **Note Refactor** for messy Zettelkasten extraction

Use it when you have raw paragraphs, ideas, fragments, or one section that should become an atomic note.

Good use:

    highlight paragraph â†’ extract to new note â†’ leave link behind

Bad use here:

    extracting complete YAML-frontmatter contract notes

Reason: frontmatter has to remain first. Note Refactor is better for prose fragments than finished contract packets.

### Use **Templater** for new blank notes

Templater is for note birth.

Use it when you want to create a fresh contract from a template:

    new contract â†’ template fills properties â†’ you write body

Do not use it when ChatGPT already gave you a fully formed note.

### Use **QuickAdd** later to automate creation

QuickAdd is the future power move.

Eventually you want commands like:

    New RateLtd Contract
    New Vouch Contract
    New Work Package
    New Handoff
    New Zettelkasten Capture

QuickAdd should call Templater and ask for:

    project
    domain
    artifact
    kind
    title

Then it creates the note in the right folder.

That is the professional workflow once the vault is stable.

### Use **Text Transporter** for moving text between existing notes

Text Transporter is useful when both notes already exist and you want to move/append/prepend content.

Good use:

    move selected paragraph to existing note
    append quote to reference note
    send task to project note

Not ideal here because these target notes do not exist yet and the frontmatter is sensitive.

### Use **Dataview** after the notes exist

Dataview is not for moving content. It is for indexing/querying.

After these notes exist, your dashboards should pick them up by:

    type: contract
    project: RateLtd
    status: active
    authority: source-of-truth

### Use **Linter** after placement

After the notes are created, lint the four new notes only.

Do **not** lint the whole vault yet.

### Use **Advanced Tables** only when editing tables

Useful for changelogs and acceptance tables. Not relevant to promotion.

### Use **Bases** later

Bases are for structured views. They are not needed to split this scratch note.

## The rule

For AI output, classify it first:

    Finished full note with frontmatter?
    â†’ create exact file manually or via file-writer

    Rough idea / paragraph / section?
    â†’ Note Refactor

    New note from scratch?
    â†’ QuickAdd + Templater

    Move text to existing note?
    â†’ Text Transporter

    Query finished notes?
    â†’ Dataview / Bases

## Finish with Git

After the four notes are created and the scratch note has linkbacks:

    git status --short
    git add -A
    git commit -m "add rateltd protocol doctrine notes"

Then open:

    obsidian open path="10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/product/rateltd.product.local-context-protocol.contract.md"

Check that the properties render cleanly. If Obsidian shows the YAML as plain text instead of properties, something is above the first `---`; fix that immediately.

## What plugin does what

### QuickAdd

Use this for the hotkey.

QuickAdd is the workflow button.

It is the thing you invoke with:

    Ctrl+Alt+N

It should run a macro.

### Templater

Use this for standards.

or:

The second one is the mature version.

    highlight a paragraph
    extract to atomic note
    leave link behind

Not ideal:

### Text Transporter

Use this when the destination note already exists.

    append this section to existing project map

Not best:

## Immediate task: do it semi-automated

Since the four note blocks already contain explicit `Vault path:` lines, the best next move is to install one reusable script into your vault.

Create this file:

    90 OBSIDIAN/Scripts/promote-selection-to-note.js

Content:

    module.exports = async function promoteSelectionToNote(params) {
      const app = params.app;
      const editor = app.workspace.activeEditor?.editor;

      if (!editor) {
        new Notice("No active editor.");
        return;
      }

      const selection = editor.getSelection();

      if (!selection || !selection.trim()) {
        new Notice("No selected text.");
        return;
      }

      const pathMatch =
        selection.match(/Vault path:\\s*`([^`]+)`/i) ||
        selection.match(/Vault path:\\s*(.+)$/im);

      if (!pathMatch) {
        new Notice("No Vault path found in selection.");
        return;
      }

      const targetPath = pathMatch[1].trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");

      if (!targetPath.endsWith(".md")) {
        new Notice("Vault path must end with .md");
        return;
      }

      const frontmatterIndex = selection.indexOf("---");

      if (frontmatterIndex === -1) {
        new Notice("No YAML frontmatter delimiter found.");
        return;
      }

      let content = selection.slice(frontmatterIndex).trimStart();

      if (!content.startsWith("---")) {
        new Notice("Content does not start with YAML frontmatter.");
        return;
      }

      const folderPath = targetPath.split("/").slice(0, -1).join("/");
      const fileBase = targetPath.split("/").pop().replace(/\\.md$/, "");

      if (folderPath && !(await app.vault.adapter.exists(folderPath))) {
        await app.vault.createFolder(folderPath);
      }

      if (await app.vault.adapter.exists(targetPath)) {
        new Notice(`Target already exists: ${targetPath}`);
        return;
      }

      await app.vault.create(targetPath, content + "\\n");

      const linkback = `Promoted to [[${fileBase}]]`;
      editor.replaceSelection(linkback);

      new Notice(`Promoted to ${fileBase}`);
    }

Then configure QuickAdd:

    Settings
    â†’ QuickAdd
    â†’ Manage Macros
    â†’ Add Macro
    â†’ name: Promote selected block to canonical note
    â†’ Add choice/action
    â†’ User Script
    â†’ select: promote-selection-to-note.js

Then:

    Settings
    â†’ Hotkeys
    â†’ search: Promote selected block to canonical note
    â†’ bind: Ctrl+Alt+N

## Working procedure after setup

For each note block:

    1. Highlight one whole Note block
    2. Press Ctrl+Alt+N
    3. Confirm it creates the target note
    4. Confirm scratch note changed to Promoted to [[...]]
    5. Repeat for next block

That is the amoeba pattern with tooling:

    capture blob
    select cell
    promote cell
    leave linkback scar
    canonical note lives in proper namespace

## For future AI output

Ask me to format notes in this promotion-packet shape:

    ## Note 1

    Vault path: `...`

    ---
    title:
    type:
    scope:
    project:
    domain:
    artifact:
    kind:
    namespace:
    status:
    authority:
    parent:
    depends_on:
    supersedes:
    tags:
    created:
    updated:
    ---

    # Title

    Body

Then your workflow is just:

    paste into capture note
    highlight one packet
    Ctrl+Alt+N
    repeat

That is the process improvement.

# Obsidian Zettelkasten Amoeba Workflow

## Purpose

Turn messy scratch notes into durable atomic notes without killing flow.

## Capture

Raw thoughts go in:

    00 ZETTELKASTEN

Capture notes may be messy. They are not required to follow contract structure.

## Extract

When a section becomes useful:

1. Highlight the useful section.
2. Use Note Composer or Note Refactor to extract it.
3. Give the new note a semantic dot-name.
4. Add basic properties.
5. Replace the source section with a link.

## Example

Source idea:

    RateLtd needs one canonical command registry. Commands should be searchable, executable, logged, and tied to PowerShell handlers.

Extracted note:

    10 PROJECTS/RateLtd/20 CONTRACTS/commands/rateltd.commands.registry.contract.md

Source note becomes:

    See [[rateltd.commands.registry.contract]]

## Rule

Scratch notes are allowed to be messy.

Contract notes are not.

    # Obsidian QuickAdd Promotion Workflow

    ## Purpose

    Promote selected scratch-note content into a canonical vault note without manually creating files, copying paths, or reformatting frontmatter.

    ## Workflow Formula

        capture â†’ select â†’ promote â†’ canonical note â†’ linkback

    ## Tool Stack

    | Tool | Role |
    |---|---|
    | Zettelkasten scratch note | Inbox / staging area |
    | QuickAdd | Command launcher |
    | QuickAdd user script | Promotion engine |
    | Templater | Standard for future generated notes |
    | Note Refactor | Rough prose extraction |
    | Text Transporter | Moving text into existing notes |
    | Dataview | Querying finished notes |
    | Linter | Cleanup after promotion |

    ## Promotion Packet Format

    Each promotable block should include:

        ## Note N

        Vault path: `target/folder/example.note.md`

        ---
        title:
        type:
        scope:
        project:
        domain:
        artifact:
        kind:
        namespace:
        status:
        authority:
        parent:
        depends_on:
        supersedes:
        tags:
        created:
        updated:
        ---

        # Note Title

        Body content.

    ## Promotion Rule

    The promoted canonical note must start at the YAML frontmatter delimiter.

    Nothing may appear above the opening:

        ---

    The following content must be stripped during promotion:

    - canvas wrapper title
    - note number heading
    - Vault path line
    - separators before frontmatter

    ## Scratch Note Linkback

    After successful promotion, the selected scratch block should be replaced with:

        Promoted to [[note.namespace]]

    ## Use QuickAdd When

    Use QuickAdd promotion when:

    - the selected block contains a declared Vault path
    - the selected block already contains full frontmatter
    - the selected block should become a canonical note
    - the source scratch note should retain only a linkback

    ## Use Note Refactor When

    Use Note Refactor when:

    - the selected text is rough prose
    - there is no declared Vault path
    - the note needs a name chosen during extraction
    - the target is an atomic idea, not a full contract packet

    ## Use Templater When

    Use Templater when:

    - creating a new blank note
    - enforcing a standard note shape
    - generating frontmatter from variables
    - building future QuickAdd-powered note factories

    ## Use Text Transporter When

    Use Text Transporter when:

    - the target note already exists
    - selected text should be moved or appended
    - the content is not creating a new canonical note

    ## Standard Operating Procedure

    1. Paste AI output into a Zettelkasten capture note.
    2. Confirm each block has a Vault path.
    3. Confirm each block has valid frontmatter.
    4. Highlight exactly one full promotion packet.
    5. Run QuickAdd command: Promote selected block to canonical note.
    6. Confirm the target note was created in the declared path.
    7. Confirm the scratch block was replaced with a linkback.
    8. Repeat for each packet.
    9. Commit the result with Git.

    ## Git Checkpoint

        git status --short
        git add -A
        git commit -m "promote zettelkasten packets into canonical notes"

    ## Rule

    Scratch notes are allowed to be messy.

    Promotion packets are allowed to be transitional.

    Canonical notes are not allowed to be dirty.

Then proceed with the script setup:

    mkdir "90 OBSIDIAN\Scripts" -Force
    notepad "90 OBSIDIAN\Scripts\promote-selection-to-note.js"

Paste the QuickAdd script there.

Then in Obsidian:

    Settings
    Community plugins
    QuickAdd
    Add Choice
    Name: Promote selected block to canonical note
    Type: Macro
    Add Choice

Then configure that macro:

    Add action
    User Script
    promote-selection-to-note.js

Then bind the command:

    Settings
    Hotkeys
    Search: Promote selected block to canonical note
    Bind: Ctrl+Alt+N

Then use it:

    Highlight one Note block
    Ctrl+Alt+N
    Verify created file
    Repeat

Your old workflow note says **what the amoeba pattern is**.

This new workflow note says **how to execute it with QuickAdd**.
